# A339 Drafting Notes

> **Navigation**: [X-Planes Drafting Notes](./README.md) | [Process](../README.md)

Article: `_drafts/x_planes_orbital_sciences_x42.markdown`, X-Planes: Orbital Sciences X-42.

These are the drafting notes removed from the article on 9 October 2026, under the pilot's rule that the
articles do not report on the drafting process and that drafting notes live in process files instead.
Each removed or changed passage is quoted below as it stood before the change, with what replaced it.

# A339 process-narrative removal (BRIEF9, 9 October 2026)

Draft `_drafts/x_planes_orbital_sciences_x42.markdown`. The full diff below is the audit record, every changed
line quoted before (`-`) and after (`+`), against the pre-edit copy of the draft. The removed gate history,
rebuild and sampling block (pre-edit lines 1186 to 1252) is quoted in full after the diff.

## Changes in brief

1. Designation section, line 135. "the reference review could not trace it further" became "no primary document stating it is available".
2. Contemporary Literature opening, line 953. The harvest, subject gate and "filter rebuild of 7 October 2026" sentence became a pointer to the admission rule in the Source Base.
3. Residual cluster description, line 979. "no attempt was made to force it below a target" became "it is not divided into invented categories to make it smaller".
4. Turbomachinery cluster description, line 1069. "an artefact of the gate" and "The queries were written around" became statements about the scope of the survey.
5. Source Base opening, line 1134. "harvested records" became "research records".
6. Source Base contemporary count, line 1140. "the count the series asks to be reported" restated as the reason for reporting the count.
7. Source Base, line 1149. The "primary-reference pass added 21 sources" paragraph, including the paragraph citation count of the earlier draft, removed.
8. Source Base, line 1154. "turned out to be the right answer ... Eight are now cited" became "supply most of the canonical relations. Eight are cited".
9. Source Base, line 1161. The "Every one of the 21 additions was verified" paragraph, including the A373 Open Library anecdote, removed.
10. Source Base, line 1174. "was found" became "is known".
11. Source Base, lines 1186 to 1252. The four gate correction rounds, the 7 October rebuild, both sampling paragraphs and the 8 October notices paragraph replaced by three present-tense paragraphs giving the admission rule, the excluded homonym and notice classes, the doubtful classes kept, and the sample contamination statement.
12. Epistemic State, lines 1385 and 1436. "during the reference review" and "the reference review failed to trace" became "has been traced" statements.
13. On Information Postdating the Editorial Date, line 1453. "the directive for this series" became "this series surveys".

````
 **24 of the 41 come from the NASA Technical Reports Server**, which is the single fact that made this
 article possible. The programme was run by a contractor under NASA management, so its engine papers,
 its incident report, its pressurant tank development and the design criteria its engineers worked from
 are all in one open register.
 
-**The primary-reference pass added 21 sources and the reason is uncomfortable.** The draft derived a
-great deal and cited almost nothing for the relations it derived from. **Sixty-three of eighty-one
-substantial paragraphs carried no citation at all.** Many were analysis that needs none, but the
-physical constants, the canonical relations and the programme context were all simply asserted.
-
-**The NASA design criteria monographs turned out to be the right answer for most of it.** Eight are now
+**The NASA design criteria monographs supply most of the canonical relations.** Eight are

 the practice this vehicle inherited rather than with the vehicle**, and they are primary documents
 rather than textbook summaries, which is why they are preferred here to a modern reference that would
 say the same thing with less authority.
 
-**Every one of the 21 additions was verified against resolved metadata rather than against a status

-against what this article cites them as. Nine identifiers were resolved through Crossref and their
-titles and authors compared. One Open Library key was resolved and its title and both authors checked,
-because a guessed key in A373 returned a perfectly healthy response for a book about motor racing.

 36th Joint Propulsion Conference in July 2000, and **reading either without the other gives a

-**The harvested corpus was gated and the gate was corrected in four rounds.** Its audit, which
-samples both what was kept and what was dropped, caught the first three. A test written afterwards
-caught the fourth.
+**The research survey admits a record only when a person reading its title finds it on this article's
+subject.** Its 9,389 records are drawn from the literature indexed by Crossref, and each is assigned to
+exactly one cluster. Much of the vocabulary this subject depends on also names something else, and a
+record that shares only such a word is excluded. Combustion chamber also names the cylinders of diesel
+and gasoline engines and the furnaces of coal and biomass boilers, catalyst bed also names the
+converters and reforming reactors of chemical process plants, tank also names road and rail tankers,
+automotive fuel tanks and civil storage tanks, booster also names child booster seats and a booster
+dose, and upper stage also names the upper playing level of the Elizabethan theatre. Natural fibre
+laminates, the wear of composites, the aqueous chemistry of dilute peroxide and hybrid electric aircraft
+propulsion are excluded as well, because they share a material, a chemical or a word with this vehicle
+and not its subject. Correction, erratum, corrigendum, retraction and withdrawal notices, figure and
+supplementary-material records, peer-review reports, journal front matter and journal indexes are
+excluded, because a survey counts research works and those are parts of works or editorial events
+rather than works, and a retracted article is not counted.
 
-**The first correction was for permissiveness.** The bare term `booster` admitted a systematic
-review of child booster seats and an essay describing defence diplomacy as needing a booster dose.
-The same audit showed the gate was simultaneously too narrow, dropping filament-wound vessel work
-because the pattern did not allow a hyphen.
-
-**The second correction was structural.** Three conjunctions had been written so that one word
-satisfied both halves, because the qualifying vocabulary list contained a word that also appeared
-in the term being qualified. Those admitted piston liner tribology and silica particle chemistry on
-the strength of a single token.
-
-**The third correction was the same defect in a fourth place, and fixing the first three had not
-found it**, because the search had been for the word rather than for the shape of the mistake. The
-qualifying list for aerospace context itself contained `booster`, so the repaired booster
-conjunction was still satisfied twice over by the one word it was meant to constrain.
+**Some records of doubtful relevance are kept.** General mechanics of filament wound tubes and of
+carbon and epoxy laminates is kept as neighbouring science for the all-composite tank, gas turbine film
+cooling as neighbouring heat-transfer science, natural fibre pressure vessels because they fall within
+the scope of the composite vessel cluster, and regenerative liquid propellant guns and ballistic impact
+on laminates because they share liquid propellant combustion or laminate damage with this vehicle. A
+reader may weigh them accordingly.
 
-**The fourth correction came from a test rather than from a sample.** Rather than hunt further
-instances, the gate acquired a check that proves the property, by asserting that no alternative in
-one half of a conjunction can match inside another half. It failed immediately on a fifth instance
-that no sample had surfaced, and passed only once that was fixed, verifying all 26 conjunctions.
-
-**The lesson is the one the gate module was written to teach, extended by one step.** A narrow gate
-reports a small corpus and reads as a thin literature. A permissive gate reports a large corpus and
-reads as thoroughness. **Only reading a random sample of both sides distinguishes those two.**
-**Sampling does not, however, find a defect whose instances do not resemble each other**, and for
-that the property has to be stated and tested rather than looked for.
-
-**The survey filter was rebuilt on 7 October 2026, after the counts first published with this
-article, and it refused 289 records.** The gate had admitted work that shares a word with this
-vehicle and nothing else. The largest group was 63 papers on diesel, gasoline and other internal
-combustion engines and their fuels, most of which entered through the phrase combustion chamber, joined by 21 on coal, biomass
-and boiler furnaces and seven on gas turbine combustors for power and industrial use. A further 45
-were chemical process catalyst beds, most of them worksheets for sulphuric acid converters and
-methane reforming reactors, which entered through the catalyst bed vocabulary of the peroxide engine.
-The rest were 33 on road and rail tankers and automotive fuel tanks, 24 on concrete, architecture
-and seismic storage tanks, 23 on unrelated process machinery such as journal bearings and solar
-water pumps, 16 on oil and gas pipelines, 16 on guns and armoured vehicles, 13 on electrical power
-engineering, 11 on ships and liquefied natural gas carriers, seven from the humanities and geology
-including two essays on the upper stage in Romeo and Juliet, six from medicine and biology, and four
-from particle accelerators and astronomy.
-
-**The research total fell from 9,748 to 9,459.** The thrust chamber cluster changed most, falling
-from 1,012 to 918, followed by the peroxide cluster at 777 to 729, the pressure-fed cluster at 576 to
-532 and the composite pressure vessel cluster at 833 to 799. The solid propulsion cluster now
-outranks the composite cluster and the nozzle cluster now outranks the pressure-fed cluster, so the
-tables above were reordered and the keystone cluster now ranks ninth. A reading of 300 unflagged
-records drawn at random found nine off topic, a rate of 3.0 percent, and all nine are among those
-refused, but that sample drove the sweeps that removed them, so it measured the contamination before
-the rebuild rather than what remained after it.
-
-**A second seeded sample of 300 records that no earlier reading had seen, drawn on 8 October 2026,
-found four off topic, and all four were removed with twelve more of their kind.** The four were a
-fire-retardant natural fibre phenolic composite, an electrically insulating epoxy, a study of hydrogen
-peroxide with iodine and iodate in aqueous solution, and a test rig for aircraft hybrid electric
-propulsion. The sweeps that followed refused three more natural fibre laminates, three wear studies of
-composites, a phenolic foam for building construction, two papers on copper complexes decomposing
-peroxide in aqueous solution, a second paper on aircraft hybrid electric propulsion, a road wheel for an
-armoured fighting vehicle and a study of German aircraft armour plate. **The research total fell from
-9,459 to 9,443.** The residual lost five records, the composite pressure vessel cluster four, the
-peroxide cluster three, and the solid propulsion and ablative clusters two each, so no cluster changed
-rank. **That sample found off-topic records at four in 300, or one in seventy-five, among records no
-earlier reading had seen**, and because it drove the sweeps that followed, it measures the contamination
-before those sweeps and not what remains after them.
-**Fifty-three more records were removed on 8 October 2026 because they are notices about works and not works**, being 33 errata, correction and corrigendum notices, 13 retraction or withdrawal notices and 7 journal indexes, under the rule that the survey counts research works and leaves out corrections, errata, retractions, withdrawals, figures, supplementary material, peer-review reports and front matter. **The research total fell from 9,443 to 9,390.** The residual lost 15 records, the peroxide and nozzle clusters six each and the solid propulsion cluster five, no cluster changed rank, and the date profile above was recomputed. On 8 October one retracted article was also removed, from the ablative cluster, which took the research total from 9,390 to 9,389 and left every share in the date profile unchanged.
+**Not every research title has been read for relevance, so the off-topic share that remains is an
+estimate from a sample.** The most recent measurement read 300 records drawn at random from those not
+previously read and found four off topic, or one in seventy-five. The records that sample exposed have
+been removed, together with others sharing their homonyms, so that figure overstates what remains by an
+unknown amount, and no later unread sample has been drawn.

 ### On Information Postdating the Editorial Date
 
 This article is written from current knowledge. Where the contemporary survey above reports work
-published after the editorial date, it is included because the directive for this series is a survey of
-the contemporary literature rather than a reconstruction of what was knowable in 2025.
+published after the editorial date, it is included because this series surveys the contemporary
+literature rather than reconstructing what was knowable in 2025.
 
 ## Out of Scope
 
````

## Removed block quoted in full (pre-edit lines 1186 to 1252)

````
**The harvested corpus was gated and the gate was corrected in four rounds.** Its audit, which
samples both what was kept and what was dropped, caught the first three. A test written afterwards
caught the fourth.

**The first correction was for permissiveness.** The bare term `booster` admitted a systematic
review of child booster seats and an essay describing defence diplomacy as needing a booster dose.
The same audit showed the gate was simultaneously too narrow, dropping filament-wound vessel work
because the pattern did not allow a hyphen.

**The second correction was structural.** Three conjunctions had been written so that one word
satisfied both halves, because the qualifying vocabulary list contained a word that also appeared
in the term being qualified. Those admitted piston liner tribology and silica particle chemistry on
the strength of a single token.

**The third correction was the same defect in a fourth place, and fixing the first three had not
found it**, because the search had been for the word rather than for the shape of the mistake. The
qualifying list for aerospace context itself contained `booster`, so the repaired booster
conjunction was still satisfied twice over by the one word it was meant to constrain.

**The fourth correction came from a test rather than from a sample.** Rather than hunt further
instances, the gate acquired a check that proves the property, by asserting that no alternative in
one half of a conjunction can match inside another half. It failed immediately on a fifth instance
that no sample had surfaced, and passed only once that was fixed, verifying all 26 conjunctions.

**The lesson is the one the gate module was written to teach, extended by one step.** A narrow gate
reports a small corpus and reads as a thin literature. A permissive gate reports a large corpus and
reads as thoroughness. **Only reading a random sample of both sides distinguishes those two.**
**Sampling does not, however, find a defect whose instances do not resemble each other**, and for
that the property has to be stated and tested rather than looked for.

**The survey filter was rebuilt on 7 October 2026, after the counts first published with this
article, and it refused 289 records.** The gate had admitted work that shares a word with this
vehicle and nothing else. The largest group was 63 papers on diesel, gasoline and other internal
combustion engines and their fuels, most of which entered through the phrase combustion chamber, joined by 21 on coal, biomass
and boiler furnaces and seven on gas turbine combustors for power and industrial use. A further 45
were chemical process catalyst beds, most of them worksheets for sulphuric acid converters and
methane reforming reactors, which entered through the catalyst bed vocabulary of the peroxide engine.
The rest were 33 on road and rail tankers and automotive fuel tanks, 24 on concrete, architecture
and seismic storage tanks, 23 on unrelated process machinery such as journal bearings and solar
water pumps, 16 on oil and gas pipelines, 16 on guns and armoured vehicles, 13 on electrical power
engineering, 11 on ships and liquefied natural gas carriers, seven from the humanities and geology
including two essays on the upper stage in Romeo and Juliet, six from medicine and biology, and four
from particle accelerators and astronomy.

**The research total fell from 9,748 to 9,459.** The thrust chamber cluster changed most, falling
from 1,012 to 918, followed by the peroxide cluster at 777 to 729, the pressure-fed cluster at 576 to
532 and the composite pressure vessel cluster at 833 to 799. The solid propulsion cluster now
outranks the composite cluster and the nozzle cluster now outranks the pressure-fed cluster, so the
tables above were reordered and the keystone cluster now ranks ninth. A reading of 300 unflagged
records drawn at random found nine off topic, a rate of 3.0 percent, and all nine are among those
refused, but that sample drove the sweeps that removed them, so it measured the contamination before
the rebuild rather than what remained after it.

**A second seeded sample of 300 records that no earlier reading had seen, drawn on 8 October 2026,
found four off topic, and all four were removed with twelve more of their kind.** The four were a
fire-retardant natural fibre phenolic composite, an electrically insulating epoxy, a study of hydrogen
peroxide with iodine and iodate in aqueous solution, and a test rig for aircraft hybrid electric
propulsion. The sweeps that followed refused three more natural fibre laminates, three wear studies of
composites, a phenolic foam for building construction, two papers on copper complexes decomposing
peroxide in aqueous solution, a second paper on aircraft hybrid electric propulsion, a road wheel for an
armoured fighting vehicle and a study of German aircraft armour plate. **The research total fell from
9,459 to 9,443.** The residual lost five records, the composite pressure vessel cluster four, the
peroxide cluster three, and the solid propulsion and ablative clusters two each, so no cluster changed
rank. **That sample found off-topic records at four in 300, or one in seventy-five, among records no
earlier reading had seen**, and because it drove the sweeps that followed, it measures the contamination
before those sweeps and not what remains after them.
**Fifty-three more records were removed on 8 October 2026 because they are notices about works and not works**, being 33 errata, correction and corrigendum notices, 13 retraction or withdrawal notices and 7 journal indexes, under the rule that the survey counts research works and leaves out corrections, errata, retractions, withdrawals, figures, supplementary material, peer-review reports and front matter. **The research total fell from 9,443 to 9,390.** The residual lost 15 records, the peroxide and nozzle clusters six each and the solid propulsion cluster five, no cluster changed rank, and the date profile above was recomputed. On 8 October one retracted article was also removed, from the ablative cluster, which took the research total from 9,390 to 9,389 and left every share in the date profile unchanged.
````

## Sources of the retained and new statements

- 9,389 research records: the Contemporary Literature table total, and the sum of the sixteen cluster rows printed by `python3 tmp/fix5/survey_tool.py stats A339` (2,281 + 916 + 809 + 792 + 720 + 696 + 605 + 542 + 529 + 520 + 273 + 211 + 201 + 148 + 75 + 71 = 9,389).
- Crossref as the source: every research link definition in the draft other than the 24 curated NTRS records and one curated web page resolves to doi.org (9,402 doi.org definitions counted with grep).
- Homonym classes: the removed block and `tmp/fix5/A339/filter.json` `patterns` and `pass2.patterns` meanings (internal combustion engines, coal and boiler furnaces, chemical process catalyst beds, road and rail tankers and automotive fuel tanks, civil storage tanks, the "upper stage in Romeo and Juliet" theatre records, the booster seat and booster dose records, natural fibre laminates, wear of composites, aqueous peroxide chemistry, aircraft hybrid electric propulsion).
- Doubtful classes kept: `filter.json` `note` (filament wound tube mechanics, carbon and epoxy laminates, gas turbine film cooling) and `pass2.patterns` notes and `tmp/fix5/A339/pass2.md` (natural fibre pressure vessels, regenerative liquid propellant guns, ballistic impact on laminates).
- Contamination statement: `filter.json` has no `pass3`. `pass2` records a 300-record sample drawn from 8,665 unread definitions, 4 off topic, 16 dropped in total. 4 in 300 is one in seventy-five. The removed block's own wording, "found four off topic", is preserved. No claim that every title was read is made.
- Removal counts by kind were dropped. The homonyms themselves are kept as present-tense exclusion rules because they describe the subject's vocabulary.

Work items T (proportion, padding review), P (primary sourcing) and Q (equation coverage), under
`tmp/fix7/BRIEF11.md`. Draft `_drafts/x_planes_orbital_sciences_x42.markdown`. The pre-edit draft is
`tmp/a339/consistency/before.markdown`; the edits are `tmp/a339/consistency/apply.py` (55 line-range
replacements, each anchored on its first and last line), `apply2.py` and `apply3.py` (exact replacements
matching once); the full unified diff is quoted at the end of this section and saved as
`tmp/a339/consistency/diff.txt`. Every new figure is computed in `tmp/a339/consistency/calc.py` and
rechecked in the new assert-based verifier `tmp/a339/verify_consistency.py` (64 checks, 0 failures;
negative tests in `tmp/a339/consistency/neg/` each fail as expected). The calculator `tmp/a339/verify.py`
was brought into line with the primary values (pre-edit copy `tmp/a339/consistency/verify.pre.py`).

## Primary documents read

- Guerrero, Hamilton, Burton, Crockett and Taylor 2004, Composite Structures 66, read in full in its
  DTIC copy ADP020323 (component of the 12th International Conference on Composite Structures,
  Melbourne, November 2003), retrieved from the Internet Archive because DTIC returned 403. Text in
  `tmp/a339/consistency/docs/ADP020323.txt`.
- Ross, Morgan, Crockett, Martinez, Anderson and McNeal 2000, AIAA 2000-3558, NTRS 20000091020, in full.
- Johnson, Anderson, Ross and Lyles 2000, AIAA 2000-3301, NTRS 20000089964, in full including the
  Program Status section (page images `j-7.png`, `j-8.png`).
- McNeal and Anderson 1999, The Peroxide Pathway, NTRS 20000002829, in full.
- Anderson, Boxwell, Crockett, Ross, Lewis, McNeal and Verdarame 1999, NTRS 20000004378, in full from page
  images. It is the same document as the hydrogen-peroxide.us "working manuscript" the article cited as
  undated, and the NTRS record dates it to the Hydrogen Peroxide Propulsion conference of November 1999
  under contract NAS8-98040.
- DeLay and Munafo 2001, NTRS 20010046957, abstract only (no PDF on NTRS).
- Baker, Greene and Frazier 2005, NTRS 20070020160, read; it is a five-page summary with no property data.
- Parsch, designation-systems.net X-42 page, re-fetched and read in full.
- Not found: the AFRL Space Vehicles release 05-17 of August 2005 that Parsch cites; the Crockett 2002
  volume-optimised tank paper Guerrero cites; the companion ASCE 2004 tankage paper (Crossref metadata
  only, no abstract, not cited). No primary source for the propellant densities was found, so they are
  labelled as assumed.

## Corrections the primary record required (P)

1. **Tank pressure.** The article inferred a maximum expected operating pressure of 733 psia with a
   1,100 psia proof, and called the opposite reading "not a design anyone would build". Guerrero 2004
   states "MEOP is 1100 PSIA", Table 1 lists a propellant tank operating pressure of 1100 psia, and
   Fig. 23 shows the proof at 1.5 x MEOP = 1650 psi. The subsection is rewritten around the stated
   value, the engine-paper floor is kept as a lower bound leaving 464 psi, and the bulkhead differential
   of 195 psid is shown to cover the 136 psi bed drop (labelled an inference). The Inference item and the
   verify.py section 7 inference are withdrawn.
2. **Stage size.** The article derived the stage's tankage from the engine's 200 s rating (93.8 ft3).
   Guerrero gives tank volumes of 44.8 and 15.5 ft3 and a 110 s stage burn. The load, fill, capacity,
   volume ratio, tank mass table, pressurant bound and velocity family are recomputed on these.
3. **Specific impulse.** The claim that "the geometry closes on" 277.8 s was a tautology, since
   c* C_F / g = F / mdot identically. The 1999 papers give the same thrust and flowrate with 278 s at
   MR 6.0 on 85 percent peroxide; Ross derives 275 s from efficiencies 0.97 and 0.98; Johnson prints
   9,480 lbf at 485 psia. The discrepancy is now explained from the record and the inference restated.
4. **Instability direction.** The article said the instability appeared as flow "fell" to one third of
   design and inferred that the fix was a precondition of the 10 and 20 percent throttling. Johnson
   records onset on ramp-up through 12 lbm/s, with stable running at about 6 lbm/s before it. The
   inference is reversed and the record's silence on ordering stated.
5. **Erosion cross-check.** The article ordered the 140 s and 31 s erosion figures by mixture ratio and
   called their agreement the strongest evidence. Ross records different injectors (configuration 2,
   steam port, against configuration 4, ring and steam port) and concludes from the 140 s test that O/F
   would need to be 4.5 or less. Both passages corrected.
6. **Contraction ratio.** "At least seven" became "about seven" (Ross section 2, Anderson 1999).
7. **Test series.** "The first development series ran through late 1998 and concluded in early 2000"
   became both campaigns spanning that period; "more than 300 seconds" became 300 seconds.
8. **Structure.** "Single piece ... eliminates the joints between tank and skirt" corrected: the tank is
   bonded into the skirt and the adhesive carries the thrust (Guerrero section 3). "Elastomeric liner"
   and "plastic liner" became fluoropolymer liners. The oxidiser liner rework is added.
9. **Dates.** December 1997 is now sourced to McNeal and Anderson 1999; the January 1998 request for
   proposals to Parsch. The "no primary document" paragraphs and the duplicated date paragraph in What
   the Record Does Not Settle are rewritten.
10. **Manuscript.** The "undated working manuscript" is NTRS 20000004378, 1999. The reference was
    re-pointed to NTRS and relabelled; the Source Base and reservation text describe it as an unfinished
    1999 paper. DeLay 2001 describes a metallic permeation barrier, not the manuscript's plastic liner;
    stated.
11. **Source Base counts.** The contemporary count was stale at 22 (23 by the existing labels); it is 24
    of 38 technical sources with the manuscript dated, and NTRS 25 of 41.
12. **Survey prose speculation** contradicting the undisclosed failure cause ("The tank failed because
    that problem was open", "a genuine limit rather than a workmanship problem") and an unsupported
    superlative ("a thrust level nothing since has matched") removed.
13. **Additions from the record**: MIS cost and storage goals, Kodiak and OSP flight plan, Bantam
    framework, Rocketdyne MIS study, engine component weight 258 lb, 102 tests and 28 minutes, the
    140 s facility limit in the 1999 paper, the chamber-life status claim, proof hold and 50 cycles with
    strain ratios, the requirements table, the schedule slip 2001, 2002, 2007, and a reservation that the
    programme's end rests on Parsch alone.
14. **Assumed Values** subsection added under Epistemic State, and each assumed value labelled where it
    is used (densities, comparison combinations, composite specific strength, thermochemical constants,
    ratios of specific heats, gas temperature, inert masses and payloads).

## Padding removed or condensed (T)

About 500 prose words of padding were removed, outweighed by the corrections and additions above. No
derivation, table, sourced fact, survey number or citation run was removed; check5 reports no orphans.
- What the Data Changed: an unsupported superlative ("largest and best-instrumented ... in the modern
  era"), an unsupported dependency claim, "composite propellant tankage went on to become ordinary", and
  "which is where its readership turned out to be".
- Dependent Systems: generic literature asides on flexible lines, slosh and the compatibility research
  condensed; "choosing against it was the programme's largest single technical risk" (speculation)
  removed; "Reading the 10 percent throttling claim without the instability paper ..." removed with the
  corrected inference.
- Comparison With Ground Prediction: the operating-point and velocity-family restatements replaced by
  pointers.
- Where the Framing Breaks Down: the fourth caution condensed.
- Epistemic State: the tank-failure and flight-silence items, already argued in full elsewhere, reduced
  to pointers; the two date paragraphs merged.
- Survey cluster prose: the infrastructure-cost sentence restated from The Flight Test Record replaced by
  a pointer.
- Conclusion: the closing designation sentence condensed.

## Equation coverage (Q), 42 candidates

- Displayed relation with the worked number, 7: the three 733-psia lines (now the stated pressure with
  the proof, burst, margin and bulkhead relations), the three throttling and instability lines (12/29.7
  and the throttle flows), and the 23 second specific impulse difference.
- Sourced and cited where first given, 19: the injector heating figures and test descriptions (Ross),
  the 150 percent proof (Guerrero), the helium tank (Anderson 1999), the facility and test-series figures
  (Ross, Anderson 1999), the hot zone (Ross) and the April 2003 proof (Guerrero, with the strain display
  added).
- Restatement in comparison, framing or conclusion, or pointer to a displayed relation, 13: the family
  span (now "by the rocket equation above"), nine Comparison With Ground Prediction lines, one Framing
  line and two Conclusion lines.
- False positives, decades, 3.
- Also displayed although not flagged: the thrust-coefficient change after erosion for 200 s and 110 s,
  the ideal specific impulse, the Johnson operating point, the nozzle-efficiency ratios, the identity
  c* C_F / g = F / mdot, and the bottle weight from DeLay's PV/W.

````diff
-### The Tank Pressure, Which Two Papers Settle Between Them
+### The Tank Pressure, and the Margin It Leaves
 
-**The tank pressure is the one number the design hinges on that neither paper states plainly.**
+**The structures paper states the tank pressure directly.** The maximum expected operating pressure of
+the propellant tanks is 1,100 pounds per square inch absolute, the hydrostatic proof test took both
+tanks to 150 percent of it, and the design carries a burst safety factor of 2.0\[[Guerrero and others 2004][research_guerrero_2004]\], so
 
-The structures paper records that the hydrostatic proof test pressurised both propellant tanks to
-150 percent of the maximum expected operating pressure, which it gives as 1,100 pounds per square inch
-absolute\[[Guerrero and others 2004][research_guerrero_2004]\]. **The sentence admits two readings.** Either 1,100 is the proof pressure, in
-which case the maximum expected operating pressure is
+$$p_{\text{proof}} = 1.5 \, p_{\text{MEOP}} = 1.5 \times 1{,}100 = 1{,}650 \ \text{psia} \qquad p_{\text{burst}} \geq 2.0 \times 1{,}100 = 2{,}200 \ \text{psia}$$
 
-$$p_{\text{MEOP}} = \frac{1100}{1.5} = 733 \ \text{psia}$$
+where MEOP is the maximum expected operating pressure.
 
-or 1,100 is the operating pressure and the proof test ran to 1,650 pounds per square inch absolute.
+**The engine paper bounds the same number from below without ever mentioning the tank.** A pressure-fed
+system must deliver propellant to the chamber against every loss between the tank and the chamber, so
 
-**The engine paper settles it without ever mentioning the tank.** A pressure-fed system must deliver
-propellant to the chamber against every loss between the tank and the chamber, so
-
 $$p_{\text{tank}} \geq p_{c} + \Delta p_{\text{bed}} + \Delta p_{\text{inj}} + \Delta p_{\text{line}}$$
 
 The chamber ran at 500 pounds per square inch absolute, and the measured catalyst bed pressure drop was
-136 pounds per square inch for the flight-like bed and 164 for the cartridge bed\[[Ross and others 2000][research_ross_2000]\].
-**That establishes a floor of 636 pounds per square inch absolute before any injector, feed line or
-flow control loss is counted at all.**
+136 pounds per square inch for the flight-like bed and 164 for the cartridge bed\[[Ross and others 2000][research_ross_2000]\]. **That establishes a
+floor of 636 pounds per square inch absolute on the oxidiser side before any injector, feed line,
+regulator or flow control loss is counted at all**, and the operating pressure leaves
 
-A maximum expected operating pressure of 733 leaves 97 pounds per square inch for the injector, the
-lines and the cavitating venturis, which is tight but ordinary. A maximum expected operating pressure of
-1,100 would leave 464 pounds per square inch for those same losses, which is most of the chamber
-pressure again and is not a design anyone would build. **The physical reading is therefore that the
-maximum expected operating pressure was 733 pounds per square inch absolute and the proof test ran to
-1,100.** This is an inference and it is marked as one in the Epistemic State, but it is an inference
-that two independent papers, written by different teams four years apart, agree on without either
-having been written to support it.
+$$1{,}100 - 636 = 464 \ \text{psi} \qquad \frac{464}{1{,}100} = 0.42$$
 
+for those losses, 42 percent of the tank pressure, to be shared by the injector, the lines, the
+regulators and the cavitating venturis described below.
+
+**The common bulkhead records the bed drop a second time.** The structures paper's test section gives
+the maximum expected pressure difference across the bulkhead as 195 pounds per square inch with the
+oxidiser tank higher and 140 with the fuel tank higher, although its requirements table lists only the
+140\[[Guerrero and others 2004][research_guerrero_2004]\]. Only the oxidiser passes through the catalyst bed, so the oxidiser tank must run above the fuel
+tank by at least the bed drop, and the larger design difference covers it,
+
+$$195 - 136 = 59 \ \text{psi}$$
+
+with room to spare. That reading is an inference, and the paper does not give the reason for the
+asymmetry.

 ### Composite pressure vessels and tank structure
 
-**This cluster is where the vehicle's actual failure lives, and it is the largest cluster whose subject the article treats in depth.** The literature divides between design and analysis of filament wound vessels, which is mature, and damage tolerance, permeation and liner behaviour, which is not. The recurring theme across the modern entries is that a composite vessel's burst strength is predictable and its behaviour after impact, cycling or contact with an aggressive fluid is much less so. **A vessel that passed a hydrostatic proof and then failed under helium is a textbook instance of that gap**, and the presence of a large and continuing literature on exactly this distinction suggests the programme met a genuine limit rather than a workmanship problem.
+**This cluster is where the vehicle's actual failure lives, and it is the largest cluster whose subject the article treats in depth.** The literature divides between design and analysis of filament wound vessels, which is mature, and damage tolerance, permeation and liner behaviour, which is not. The recurring theme across the modern entries is that a composite vessel's burst strength is predictable and its behaviour after impact, cycling or contact with an aggressive fluid is much less so. **A vessel that passed a hydrostatic proof and then failed under helium sits squarely in that gap**, and the public record does not say whether it met a limit of the technology or a fault of manufacture.

 ### Hydrogen peroxide and green propellant propulsion
 
-**The striking feature of this cluster is its date distribution rather than its size.** Concentrated peroxide was abandoned as a propellant in the 1960s and the literature reflects that, thinning almost to nothing and then reviving. The revival is driven by the regulatory and handling cost of hydrazine rather than by any new physics, and the modern entries are dominated by small satellite and cubesat propulsion, hybrid rocket oxidisers and catalyst formulation. **The Upper Stage Flight Experiment sits at the start of that revival and at a thrust level nothing since has matched**, which makes its test data unusually valuable and its cancellation unusually costly.
+**The striking feature of this cluster is its date distribution rather than its size.** Concentrated peroxide was abandoned as a propellant in the 1960s and the literature reflects that, thinning almost to nothing and then reviving.  ### Test facilities, instrumentation and diagnostics
 
-**Second smallest in the table and, for its size, the one with the most direct bearing on how the programme ended.** Concentrated peroxide requires dedicated facilities, cleaning protocols and materials controls, and the published record includes both an account of building that capability at the Stennis Space Center and an account of an incident there. **A propellant whose infrastructure is itself a research topic is not a low cost propellant in the short run**, whatever its unit price, and this cluster is where that cost is visible.

 **The upper stage cluster is the mirror image and it is the bleakest number in the table.** It carries one of the largest pre-2000 shares of any central cluster at 38 percent and is the smallest central cluster in absolute terms. **The pop-up insertion mission was posed, worked on, and then abandoned without being solved**, and the modern entries under this heading serve commercial rideshare rather than the military responsive-insertion mission that justified this vehicle.
 
-**The cluster covering the technology that actually killed the programme is among the youngest of the central clusters.** Composite pressure vessels stand at 52 percent post-2015, behind only the ablative cluster at 54 percent and the peroxide cluster at 53 among the nine, although three smaller clusters outside that set are younger still, launch vehicle dynamics and turbomachinery at 59 percent and cryogenics at 56. **The tank failed because that problem was open, and the date profile says it is open still.** A reader looking for the part of this vehicle that has a future should look there rather than at the stage.
+**The cluster covering the technology that actually ended the programme is among the youngest of the central clusters.** Composite pressure vessels stand at 52 percent post-2015, behind only the ablative cluster at 54 percent and the peroxide cluster at 53 among the nine, although three smaller clusters outside that set are younger still, launch vehicle dynamics and turbomachinery at 59 percent and cryogenics at 56. **The date profile says that the field the tank belongs to is still active.** A reader looking for the part of this vehicle that has a future should look there rather than at the stage.
 
 ## Where the Framing Breaks Down

 pressure-fed choice was survivable and not why it was made**, and those are different questions.
 
 **Second, the relation treats the tank as a membrane and the failure was not a membrane failure.** A
-vessel that passes a hydrostatic proof at 150 percent and then fails during a helium pressurisation is
-telling you about liners, joints, bosses, permeation, or the difference between a liquid and a gas as
-the pressurising medium. **None of those appear anywhere in the keystone**, which is precisely why the
-keystone cannot explain the outcome of the programme.
+vessel that passes a hydrostatic proof at 150 percent and fifty cycles to its operating pressure and
+then fails during a helium pressurisation points to liners, joints, bosses, permeation, the adhesive
+bond, or the difference between a liquid and a gas as the pressurising medium. **None of those appear
+anywhere in the keystone**, which is precisely why the keystone cannot explain the outcome of the
+programme.
 
 **Third, treating the vehicle as a technology demonstrator understates how much of it was ordinary.**

+1999 table pairs the same thrust and flowrate with 278 seconds, which closes, and the 2000 table changed
+the specific impulse and the mixture ratio and left thrust and flowrate as they were. Neither paper says
+so.

+family into an answer**, and it is precisely the quantity a cancelled programme never publishes.

 ### On Sources Used With Reservation
 
-**One source used in the Dependent Systems section is a working manuscript rather than a publication.**
-It carries the correct author set, the contract number and technical content consistent with the
-published papers, and it also carries visible drafting artefacts including an unwritten section and an
-uncorrected edit. **It is the only source for several subsystem details**, being the nitrogen attitude
-control tank pressure, the linear shaped charge separation arrangement and the propellant management
-thrusters. Those details are reported here as coming from it, and the helium pressurant tank it
-describes is independently corroborated\[[DeLay and Munafo 2001][research_delay_2001]\]. **A reader should
-treat the uncorroborated subsystem figures as the weakest quantitative claims in this article.**
+**One source used in the Dependent Systems section is an unfinished paper.** NASA's Technical Reports
+Server holds it as a 1999 paper under contract NAS8-98040, and it carries an unwritten section, an
+uncorrected edit and an editorial note to a co-author. **It is the only source for several subsystem
+details**, being the nitrogen attitude control tank pressure, the separation arrangement and the
+propellant management thrusters. Its helium pressurant tank is corroborated\[[DeLay and Munafo 2001][research_delay_2001]\], although the
+corroborating paper describes a metallic permeation barrier where this one describes a plastic liner.
+**A reader should treat the uncorroborated subsystem figures as the weakest quantitative claims in this
+article.**
+
+**The end of the programme rests on one secondary source.** The tank failure, the second prototype's
+leakage and the 2006 and 2007 schedule appear only in the designation survey\[[USFE X-42][ref_x42_parsch]\], which cites an Air Force
+Research Laboratory release of August 2005 that is not available here.
+
+### Assumed Values
+
+**These values are not stated by any source cited here and are assumed for the derivations.** They are
+the propellant densities of 1.387 grams per cubic centimetre for 90 percent peroxide and 0.804 for JP-8,
+the bulk densities and specific impulses of the three comparison combinations in the density impulse
+table, the specific strength range of $1.0 \times 10^{6}$ to $1.6 \times 10^{6}$ joules per kilogram for
+filament wound carbon and epoxy, the reaction enthalpy, latent heat and heat capacities of the
+decomposition balance, the ratios of specific heats of 1.20 and 1.25 for the combustion products and 1.4
+for nitrogen, the gas temperature of 300 kelvin for helium and nitrogen, and the inert masses and
+payloads of the velocity family.
````
